========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hello, Doctor. Thank you for seeing me, I really appreciate your time. I hope I'm not a bother to you today. 

Well, um... my family is actually the one who wanted me here. They're a little upset with me, I suppose. They say I’m here because I... well, I dipped into our savings a bit. It was just to buy some wood, and a bunch of lights, and some display stuff for the yard. I know it probably sounds irresponsible to them, but I was just trying to do something really nice. 

I kept pushing the work after dark, just trying to get it all set up, because... well, I felt like if I stopped, the whole house, and really the whole block, would just lose its chance. It felt like such an important thing to finish for everyone's sake. I was just trying to do something good for the neighborhood, you know? I hope you don't think badly of me for wanting to help out... does that make sense, Doctor?

Well, I... I really don't want to sound boastful, Doctor. I know nobody likes a show-off, and I promise I'm not trying to be one. But... yes, I suppose I did think that.

I just... well, I feel like nobody else really understands light and pictures the exact way I do. I can just see how it all goes together so perfectly in my head, and I wanted to share that gift.

Well, um... I suppose I've always liked to tinker a bit, you know? Just little things around the house. But... no, I wouldn't say I usually take on things quite this big.

It's just... lately, this idea just felt so right, and I had so much energy for it. I didn't even feel tired, even when I was working late. It just felt like a special occasion, I guess. I hope that's alright? I mean, it's not a bad thing to find a new passion at my age, is it?

Well, I... I suppose there have been a few little things, but nothing too terrible, I hope. I mean, mostly I've just been feeling... well, really wonderful, to be honest. Most days I wake up feeling so charged up, almost joyful, you know? Like I'm a young man again, ready to take on the world. It's been a lovely feeling, really. 

But... oh, I hate to admit this, Doctor, because I really do try to be a patient man... I guess I have been a little short-tempered lately. Just... just when people don't understand what I'm trying to do. Like if my wife asks too many questions about the project, or those contractors I hired try to tell me something can't be done... I just get so hot, so fast. 

I've even yelled a few times, and I feel just awful about it afterwards. I know it's not polite to raise your voice, and I really don't want to be a difficult person to be around. Well, I... I know people probably think I'm a bit mixed up in the head right now, and I really hope you don't think I'm crazy for saying this. I just... I want to be honest with you, because you've been so kind. 

I don't think I'm a prophet or anything grand like that, I promise. But... well, I am quite sure that our neighborhood was picked for something special. Like... like it's meant to be the site of my last big civic art piece. The yard, the lights, all of it. It's supposed to bring everyone together, I think. 

And I know it sounds silly, an old man talking like this, but... I see little signs that other people know it, too. Like when the delivery trucks slow down as they drive past, or when the neighbors peek over the fence. They don't say anything, but I can tell they understand something important is happening. 

I hope that doesn't sound too strange? I just feel like I'm finally doing something that really matters, and I'd hate for you to think I'm just losing my marbles. Do you... do you think it's okay to feel that way?

I know it probably sounds irresponsible to them, but I was just trying to do something really nice. \n\nI kept pushing the work after dark, just trying to get it all set up, because... well, I felt like if I stopped, the whole house, and really the whole block, would just lose its chance. It felt like such an important thing to finish for everyone's sake. I was just trying to do something good for the neighborhood, you know? I hope you don't think badly of me for wanting to help out... does that make sense, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like your yard display was going to be the best on the block or even in the whole town?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I really don't want to sound boastful, Doctor. I know nobody likes a show-off, and I promise I'm not trying to be one. But... yes, I suppose I did think that. \n\nI honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something really important. You know, something people would actually travel just to come see. I just... well, I feel like nobody else really understands light and pictures the exact way I do. I can just see how it all goes together so perfectly in my head, and I wanted to share that gift. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound too arrogant of me to say that out loud to you... is it okay to feel a little proud of a talent like that? I just wanted to do a good job.\n\nDoctor: Have you always been someone who comes up with big projects like this, or is this a relatively new thing for you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose I've always liked to tinker a bit, you know? Just little things around the house. But... no, I wouldn't say I usually take on things quite this big. \n\nI mean, I'm 73, Doctor. I usually just like to sit on the porch and enjoy a quiet afternoon. I don't want you to think I'm always running around causing a fuss or spending money we don't have. I'm usually very careful, I promise. \n\nIt's just... lately, this idea just felt so right, and I had so much energy for it. I didn't even feel tired, even when I was working late. It just felt like a special occasion, I guess. I hope that's alright? I mean, it's not a bad thing to find a new passion at my age, is it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your ideas or abilities are superior to others?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that I'm some sort of... well, a know-it-all. I would hate for you to think that about me. \n\nI honestly don't think I'm better than anyone else. I mean, I didn't even finish high school, so I've always known I'm not the smartest fellow in the room. Usually, I'm just happy to take a back seat and listen to what other folks have to say. \n\nIt's really just... just this one time, with the yard and the lights, that I felt like I had a special touch for something. But normally? No, Doctor, I would never look down on my neighbors or think I'm superior to anybody. I've always tried to be a good, humble person. I hope you believe me when I say that?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your mood or behavior over the past few months, aside from your enthusiasm for the yard project?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose there have been a few little things, but nothing too terrible, I hope. I mean, mostly I've just been feeling... well, really wonderful, to be honest. Most days I wake up feeling so charged up, almost joyful, you know? Like I'm a young man again, ready to take on the world. It's been a lovely feeling, really. \n\nBut... oh, I hate to admit this, Doctor, because I really do try to be a patient man... I guess I have been a little short-tempered lately. Just... just when people don't understand what I'm trying to do. Like if my wife asks too many questions about the project, or those contractors I hired try to tell me something can't be done... I just get so hot, so fast. \n\nI've even yelled a few times, and I feel just awful about it afterwards. I know it's not polite to raise your voice, and I really don't want to be a difficult person to be around. Do you think it's normal to get a little frustrated when you have a clear vision and people keep getting in the way? I'm trying to be better about it, I really am.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you didn't feel wonderful or charged up?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, not really. I mean, I've been feeling pretty good overall. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around moping or anything like that. I've always tried to keep a positive attitude, you know? \n\nI suppose... well, everyone has a quiet day now and then, right? Maybe a day where I just sit and rest a bit more, but nothing bad. I certainly haven't been feeling down or depressed, if that's what you mean. I'm usually very cheerful. I hope that's the right answer? I just wouldn't want you to worry that I'm not taking care of myself emotionally. I've been doing just fine in that department, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have a special role or mission in life that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I know people probably think I'm a bit mixed up in the head right now, and I really hope you don't think I'm crazy for saying this. I just... It's supposed to bring everyone together, I think. \n\nAnd I know it sounds silly, an old man talking like this, but... I see little signs that other people know it, too. Like when the delivery trucks slow down as they drive past, or when the neighbors peek over the fence. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, my... "powers" sounds a bit like a comic book, doesn't it? I wouldn't use a word like that, I promise. I don't want you to think I've lost touch with reality or believe I can fly or anything silly like that. 

It's just... well, I hope this doesn't sound too boastful. I was always taught to be humble, and I really try not to brag, Doctor, I do. But... I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something really important. Something that folks will actually travel from all over just to come see. 

And it's mostly because... well, I've just realized that nobody understands light and pictures quite the way I do. It all just makes perfect sense to me right now, clear as a bell. I have this... this eye for it, I suppose, that sets me apart when it comes to this work. 

I hope it's alright to say that? I really don't want to come across as conceited to you, I'd hate for you to think poorly of me. I just feel so certain about what I can build. It's perfectly normal to be a little proud when you discover you have a special talent for something, isn't it?

Well, yes... I do still feel that way. I mean, I hope you don't think I'm being too big for my britches, saying it again. I know I shouldn't toot my own horn too much, and I really try to be modest, I do.

I hope you don't think I'm being arrogant? I'd hate for you to think I'm just an old man full of hot air. I just feel so sure about it, and I want to be honest with you. It's okay to have a little faith in your own abilities, isn't it?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, "greatness"... that's a very strong word, Doctor. I really hope I'm not giving you the impression that I think I'm some sort of famous celebrity or a king or anything. I'd be so embarrassed if you thought I was getting a big head. I was always taught that pride comes before a fall, you know?

It's really just like I was trying to explain a moment ago. I know I keep repeating myself, and I'm so sorry if I'm talking your ear off about it. But... I just feel tied to that Schiller Park house project. I don't think I'm destined to rule the world, but I do honestly believe I'm meant to turn that specific property into something truly important. 

Not because *I'm* great, but just because... well, like I was saying, the way I see the lights and the pictures in my mind right now, it's just so perfectly clear. Nobody else quite has that same vision or understands it the way I do right now. And I just want to put it together so people can come see it and feel it, too. 

Does that make sense to you? I just want to do something beautiful for the community while I still can. I really hope you don't think I'm being too full of myself for feeling that way?

Well, yes, Doctor, I do think about it every day. I really hope you don't mind me talking about it so much. I know my family and some of the folks around here think I'm a little... mixed up, maybe. But... I'm just so sure that this neighborhood was picked for my last big civic art piece. I don't mean that in a boastful way, I promise. It's just... well, I can tell people know about it, too. Sometimes a delivery truck will slow down right as it goes past the house, or I'll catch a neighbor looking over the fence. It just proves they can sense something important is happening there. 

I hope it doesn't sound too strange when I say that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers. It's perfectly normal to notice when people are paying attention to your hard work, isn't it? I just want to do a good job for the community.

They say I’m here because I... well, I dipped into our savings a bit. It was just to buy some wood, and a bunch of lights, and some display stuff for the yard. I know it probably sounds irresponsible to them, but I was just trying to do something really nice. \n\nI kept pushing the work after dark, just trying to get it all set up, because... well, I felt like if I stopped, the whole house, and really the whole block, would just lose its chance. It felt like such an important thing to finish for everyone's sake. I was just trying to do something good for the neighborhood, you know? I hope you don't think badly of me for wanting to help out... does that make sense, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like your yard display was going to be the best on the block or even in the whole town?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I really don't want to sound boastful, Doctor. I know nobody likes a show-off, and I promise I'm not trying to be one. But... yes, I suppose I did think that. \n\nI honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something really important. You know, something people would actually travel just to come see. I just... well, I feel like nobody else really understands light and pictures the exact way I do. I can just see how it all goes together so perfectly in my head, and I wanted to share that gift. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound too arrogant of me to say that out loud to you... is it okay to feel a little proud of a talent like that? I just wanted to do a good job.\n\nDoctor: Have you always been someone who comes up with big projects like this, or is this a relatively new thing for you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose I've always liked to tinker a bit, you know? Just little things around the house. But... no, I wouldn't say I usually take on things quite this big. \n\nI mean, I'm 73, Doctor. I usually just like to sit on the porch and enjoy a quiet afternoon. I don't want you to think I'm always running around causing a fuss or spending money we don't have. I'm usually very careful, I promise. \n\nIt's just... lately, this idea just felt so right, and I had so much energy for it. I didn't even feel tired, even when I was working late. It just felt like a special occasion, I guess. I hope that's alright? I mean, it's not a bad thing to find a new passion at my age, is it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your ideas or abilities are superior to others?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that I'm some sort of... well, a know-it-all. I would hate for you to think that about me. \n\nI honestly don't think I'm better than anyone else. I mean, I didn't even finish high school, so I've always known I'm not the smartest fellow in the room. Usually, I'm just happy to take a back seat and listen to what other folks have to say. \n\nIt's really just... just this one time, with the yard and the lights, that I felt like I had a special touch for something. But normally? No, Doctor, I would never look down on my neighbors or think I'm superior to anybody. I've always tried to be a good, humble person. I hope you believe me when I say that?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your mood or behavior over the past few months, aside from your enthusiasm for the yard project?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose there have been a few little things, but nothing too terrible, I hope. I mean, mostly I've just been feeling... well, really wonderful, to be honest. Most days I wake up feeling so charged up, almost joyful, you know? Like I'm a young man again, ready to take on the world. It's been a lovely feeling, really. \n\nBut... oh, I hate to admit this, Doctor, because I really do try to be a patient man... I guess I have been a little short-tempered lately. Just... just when people don't understand what I'm trying to do. Like if my wife asks too many questions about the project, or those contractors I hired try to tell me something can't be done... I just get so hot, so fast. \n\nI've even yelled a few times, and I feel just awful about it afterwards. I know it's not polite to raise your voice, and I really don't want to be a difficult person to be around. Do you think it's normal to get a little frustrated when you have a clear vision and people keep getting in the way? I'm trying to be better about it, I really am.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you didn't feel wonderful or charged up?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, not really. I mean, I've been feeling pretty good overall. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around moping or anything like that. I've always tried to keep a positive attitude, you know? \n\nI suppose... well, everyone has a quiet day now and then, right? Maybe a day where I just sit and rest a bit more, but nothing bad. I certainly haven't been feeling down or depressed, if that's what you mean. I'm usually very cheerful. I hope that's the right answer? I just wouldn't want you to worry that I'm not taking care of myself emotionally. I've been doing just fine in that department, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have a special role or mission in life that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I know people probably think I'm a bit mixed up in the head right now, and I really hope you don't think I'm crazy for saying this. I just... I want to be honest with you, because you've been so kind. \n\nI don't think I'm a prophet or anything grand like that, I promise. But... well, I am quite sure that our neighborhood was picked for something special. Like... like it's meant to be the site of my last big civic art piece. The yard, the lights, all of it. It's supposed to bring everyone together, I think. \n\nAnd I know it sounds silly, an old man talking like this, but... I see little signs that other people know it, too. Like when the delivery trucks slow down as they drive past, or when the neighbors peek over the fence. They don't say anything, but I can tell they understand something important is happening. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too strange? I just feel like I'm finally doing something that really matters, and I'd hate for you to think I'm just losing my marbles. Do you... do you think it's okay to feel that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have any special powers or abilities that set you apart from others?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... \"powers\" sounds a bit like a comic book, doesn't it? I wouldn't use a word like that, I promise. I don't want you to think I've lost touch with reality or believe I can fly or anything silly like that. \n\nIt's just... well, I hope this doesn't sound too boastful. I was always taught to be humble, and I really try not to brag, Doctor, I do. But... I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something really important. Something that folks will actually travel from all over just to come see. \n\nAnd it's mostly because... well, I've just realized that nobody understands light and pictures quite the way I do. It all just makes perfect sense to me right now, clear as a bell. I have this... this eye for it, I suppose, that sets me apart when it comes to this work. \n\nI hope it's alright to say that? I really don't want to come across as conceited to you, I'd hate for you to think poorly of me. I just feel so certain about what I can build. It's perfectly normal to be a little proud when you discover you have a special talent for something, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel like your yard project is going to be something that people will travel to see?\n\nPatient: Well, yes... I do still feel that way. I mean, I hope you don't think I'm being too big for my britches, saying it again. I know I shouldn't toot my own horn too much, and I really try to be modest, I do. \n\nBut... yes, I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something truly important. Something folks will want to come see from all over. It's just... well, like I mentioned before, I just feel like nobody else understands how to put the light and the pictures together quite the way I do right now. It's like a gift, I suppose. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm being arrogant? I'd hate for you to think I'm just an old man full of hot air. I just feel so sure about it, and I want to be honest with you. It's okay to have a little faith in your own abilities, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're destined for greatness or have a special connection to something bigger than yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, \"greatness\"... that's a very strong word, Doctor. I really hope I'm not giving you the impression that I think I'm some sort of famous celebrity or a king or anything. I'd be so embarrassed if you thought I was getting a big head. I was always taught that pride comes before a fall, you know?\n\nIt's really just like I was trying to explain a moment ago. I know I keep repeating myself, and I'm so sorry if I'm talking your ear off about it. But... I just feel tied to that Schiller Park house project. I don't think I'm destined to rule the world, but I do honestly believe I'm meant to turn that specific property into something truly important. \n\nNot because *I'm* great, but just because... well, like I was saying, the way I see the lights and the pictures in my mind right now, it's just so perfectly clear. Nobody else quite has that same vision or understands it the way I do right now. And I just want to put it together so people can come see it and feel it, too. \n\nDoes that make sense to you? I just want to do something beautiful for the community while I still can. I really hope you don't think I'm being too full of myself for feeling that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you still think about your yard project and its potential impact on the community every day?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, Doctor, I do think about it every day. I really hope you don't mind me talking about it so much. I know my family and some of the folks around here think I'm a little... mixed up, maybe. And I'd hate for you to think I'm just an old fool causing a fuss for no reason. I always try to be a good neighbor, I really do.\n\nBut... I'm just so sure that this neighborhood was picked for my last big civic art piece. I don't mean that in a boastful way, I promise. It's just... well, I can tell people know about it, too. Sometimes a delivery truck will slow down right as it goes past the house, or I'll catch a neighbor looking over the fence. It just proves they can sense something important is happening there. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound too strange when I say that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers. It's perfectly normal to notice when people are paying attention to your hard work, isn't it? I just want to do a good job for the community.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't feel like you had a special role or mission in life?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose so. I mean, I haven't always felt quite this... connected to things, if that makes sense. I hope you don't think I've been walking around my whole life thinking I was on some grand crusade. I've always just tried to be a regular, hardworking fellow, you know? Pay my bills, keep my nose clean. \n\nBefore I got so charged up with these remodeling plans for the house, I wasn't really... well, I wasn't seeing any special meaning in the delivery trucks slowing down or the neighbors looking over the fence. Those sorts of things... those signs, I guess you could call them... they really only started happening once I felt so driven to turn the Schiller Park house into my big civic project. \n\nIt was like a switch flipped, and suddenly it all just became so clear to me. But before that, no, I didn't feel like I had a special mission. I was just an ordinary old man. I hope it's okay that I feel this way now? I just want to make sure I'm not sounding too foolish to you. It's alright for a person to find their true calling a little later in life, isn't it?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I've always just tried to be a regular, hardworking fellow, you know? Well... yes, I suppose I do still believe that. I really hope that doesn't make me sound arrogant or... or boastful. I know I don't have a fancy education or anything, and I'd hate for you to think I'm putting on airs.

It just felt like a special occasion, I guess. I hope that's alright? I mean, it's not a bad thing to find a new passion at my age, is it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your ideas or abilities are superior to others?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that I'm some sort of... well, a know-it-all. I would hate for you to think that about me. \n\nI honestly don't think I'm better than anyone else. I mean, I didn't even finish high school, so I've always known I'm not the smartest fellow in the room. Usually, I'm just happy to take a back seat and listen to what other folks have to say. \n\nIt's really just... just this one time, with the yard and the lights, that I felt like I had a special touch for something. But normally? No, Doctor, I would never look down on my neighbors or think I'm superior to anybody. I've always tried to be a good, humble person. I hope you believe me when I say that?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your mood or behavior over the past few months, aside from your enthusiasm for the yard project?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose there have been a few little things, but nothing too terrible, I hope. I mean, mostly I've just been feeling... well, really wonderful, to be honest. Most days I wake up feeling so charged up, almost joyful, you know? Like I'm a young man again, ready to take on the world. It's been a lovely feeling, really. \n\nBut... oh, I hate to admit this, Doctor, because I really do try to be a patient man... I guess I have been a little short-tempered lately. Just... just when people don't understand what I'm trying to do. Like if my wife asks too many questions about the project, or those contractors I hired try to tell me something can't be done... I just get so hot, so fast. \n\nI've even yelled a few times, and I feel just awful about it afterwards. I know it's not polite to raise your voice, and I really don't want to be a difficult person to be around. Do you think it's normal to get a little frustrated when you have a clear vision and people keep getting in the way? I'm trying to be better about it, I really am.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you didn't feel wonderful or charged up?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, not really. I mean, I've been feeling pretty good overall. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around moping or anything like that. I've always tried to keep a positive attitude, you know? \n\nI suppose... well, everyone has a quiet day now and then, right? Maybe a day where I just sit and rest a bit more, but nothing bad. I certainly haven't been feeling down or depressed, if that's what you mean. I'm usually very cheerful. I hope that's the right answer? I just wouldn't want you to worry that I'm not taking care of myself emotionally. I've been doing just fine in that department, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have a special role or mission in life that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I know people probably think I'm a bit mixed up in the head right now, and I really hope you don't think I'm crazy for saying this. I just... I want to be honest with you, because you've been so kind. \n\nI don't think I'm a prophet or anything grand like that, I promise. But... well, I am quite sure that our neighborhood was picked for something special. Like... like it's meant to be the site of my last big civic art piece. The yard, the lights, all of it. It's supposed to bring everyone together, I think. \n\nAnd I know it sounds silly, an old man talking like this, but... I see little signs that other people know it, too. Like when the delivery trucks slow down as they drive past, or when the neighbors peek over the fence. They don't say anything, but I can tell they understand something important is happening. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too strange? I just feel like I'm finally doing something that really matters, and I'd hate for you to think I'm just losing my marbles. I wouldn't use a word like that, I promise. I don't want you to think I've lost touch with reality or believe I can fly or anything silly like that. \n\nIt's just... well, I hope this doesn't sound too boastful. I was always taught to be humble, and I really try not to brag, Doctor, I do. But... I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something really important. Something that folks will actually travel from all over just to come see. \n\nAnd it's mostly because... well, I've just realized that nobody understands light and pictures quite the way I do. It all just makes perfect sense to me right now, clear as a bell. I have this... this eye for it, I suppose, that sets me apart when it comes to this work. \n\nI hope it's alright to say that? I really don't want to come across as conceited to you, I'd hate for you to think poorly of me. I just feel so certain about what I can build. It's perfectly normal to be a little proud when you discover you have a special talent for something, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel like your yard project is going to be something that people will travel to see?\n\nPatient: Well, yes... I do still feel that way. I mean, I hope you don't think I'm being too big for my britches, saying it again. I know I shouldn't toot my own horn too much, and I really try to be modest, I do. \n\nBut... yes, I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something truly important. Something folks will want to come see from all over. It's just... well, like I mentioned before, I just feel like nobody else understands how to put the light and the pictures together quite the way I do right now. It's like a gift, I suppose. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm being arrogant? I'd hate for you to think I'm just an old man full of hot air. I just feel so sure about it, and I want to be honest with you. I was always taught that pride comes before a fall, you know?\n\nIt's really just like I was trying to explain a moment ago. I don't think I'm destined to rule the world, but I do honestly believe I'm meant to turn that specific property into something truly important. \n\nNot because *I'm* great, but just because... well, like I was saying, the way I see the lights and the pictures in my mind right now, it's just so perfectly clear. Nobody else quite has that same vision or understands it the way I do right now. And I just want to put it together so people can come see it and feel it, too. \n\nDoes that make sense to you? I just want to do something beautiful for the community while I still can. I really hope you don't think I'm being too full of myself for feeling that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you still think about your yard project and its potential impact on the community every day?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, Doctor, I do think about it every day. I mean, I didn't even finish high school, so I've always known I'm not the smartest fellow in the room. Usually, I'm just happy to take a back seat and listen to what other folks have to say. \n\nIt's really just... just this one time, with the yard and the lights, that I felt like I had a special touch for something. But normally? No, Doctor, I would never look down on my neighbors or think I'm superior to anybody. I've always tried to be a good, humble person. I hope you believe me when I say that?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your mood or behavior over the past few months, aside from your enthusiasm for the yard project?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose there have been a few little things, but nothing too terrible, I hope. I mean, mostly I've just been feeling... well, really wonderful, to be honest. Most days I wake up feeling so charged up, almost joyful, you know? Like I'm a young man again, ready to take on the world. It's been a lovely feeling, really. \n\nBut... oh, I hate to admit this, Doctor, because I really do try to be a patient man... I guess I have been a little short-tempered lately. Just... just when people don't understand what I'm trying to do. Like if my wife asks too many questions about the project, or those contractors I hired try to tell me something can't be done... I just get so hot, so fast. \n\nI've even yelled a few times, and I feel just awful about it afterwards. I know it's not polite to raise your voice, and I really don't want to be a difficult person to be around. Do you think it's normal to get a little frustrated when you have a clear vision and people keep getting in the way? I'm trying to be better about it, I really am.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you didn't feel wonderful or charged up?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, not really. I mean, I've been feeling pretty good overall. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around moping or anything like that. I've always tried to keep a positive attitude, you know? \n\nI suppose... well, everyone has a quiet day now and then, right? Maybe a day where I just sit and rest a bit more, but nothing bad. I certainly haven't been feeling down or depressed, if that's what you mean. I'm usually very cheerful. I hope that's the right answer? I just wouldn't want you to worry that I'm not taking care of myself emotionally. I've been doing just fine in that department, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have a special role or mission in life that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I know people probably think I'm a bit mixed up in the head right now, and I really hope you don't think I'm crazy for saying this. I just... I want to be honest with you, because you've been so kind. \n\nI don't think I'm a prophet or anything grand like that, I promise. But... well, I am quite sure that our neighborhood was picked for something special. Like... like it's meant to be the site of my last big civic art piece. The yard, the lights, all of it. It's supposed to bring everyone together, I think. \n\nAnd I know it sounds silly, an old man talking like this, but... I see little signs that other people know it, too. Like when the delivery trucks slow down as they drive past, or when the neighbors peek over the fence. They don't say anything, but I can tell they understand something important is happening. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too strange? I just feel like I'm finally doing something that really matters, and I'd hate for you to think I'm just losing my marbles. Do you... do you think it's okay to feel that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have any special powers or abilities that set you apart from others?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... \"powers\" sounds a bit like a comic book, doesn't it? I wouldn't use a word like that, I promise. I don't want you to think I've lost touch with reality or believe I can fly or anything silly like that. \n\nIt's just... well, I hope this doesn't sound too boastful. I was always taught to be humble, and I really try not to brag, Doctor, I do. But... I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something really important. Something that folks will actually travel from all over just to come see. \n\nAnd it's mostly because... well, I've just realized that nobody understands light and pictures quite the way I do. It all just makes perfect sense to me right now, clear as a bell. I have this... this eye for it, I suppose, that sets me apart when it comes to this work. \n\nI hope it's alright to say that? I really don't want to come across as conceited to you, I'd hate for you to think poorly of me. I just feel so certain about what I can build. It's perfectly normal to be a little proud when you discover you have a special talent for something, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel like your yard project is going to be something that people will travel to see?\n\nPatient: Well, yes... I do still feel that way. I mean, I hope you don't think I'm being too big for my britches, saying it again. I know I shouldn't toot my own horn too much, and I really try to be modest, I do. \n\nBut... yes, I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something truly important. Something folks will want to come see from all over. It's just... well, like I mentioned before, I just feel like nobody else understands how to put the light and the pictures together quite the way I do right now. It's like a gift, I suppose. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm being arrogant? I'd hate for you to think I'm just an old man full of hot air. I just feel so sure about it, and I want to be honest with you. It's okay to have a little faith in your own abilities, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're destined for greatness or have a special connection to something bigger than yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, \"greatness\"... that's a very strong word, Doctor. I really hope I'm not giving you the impression that I think I'm some sort of famous celebrity or a king or anything. I'd be so embarrassed if you thought I was getting a big head. I was always taught that pride comes before a fall, you know?\n\nIt's really just like I was trying to explain a moment ago. I know I keep repeating myself, and I'm so sorry if I'm talking your ear off about it. But... I just feel tied to that Schiller Park house project. I don't think I'm destined to rule the world, but I do honestly believe I'm meant to turn that specific property into something truly important. \n\nNot because *I'm* great, but just because... well, like I was saying, the way I see the lights and the pictures in my mind right now, it's just so perfectly clear. Nobody else quite has that same vision or understands it the way I do right now. And I just want to put it together so people can come see it and feel it, too. \n\nDoes that make sense to you? I just want to do something beautiful for the community while I still can. I really hope you don't think I'm being too full of myself for feeling that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you still think about your yard project and its potential impact on the community every day?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, Doctor, I do think about it every day. I really hope you don't mind me talking about it so much. I know my family and some of the folks around here think I'm a little... mixed up, maybe. And I'd hate for you to think I'm just an old fool causing a fuss for no reason. I always try to be a good neighbor, I really do.\n\nBut... I'm just so sure that this neighborhood was picked for my last big civic art piece. I don't mean that in a boastful way, I promise. It's just... well, I can tell people know about it, too. Sometimes a delivery truck will slow down right as it goes past the house, or I'll catch a neighbor looking over the fence. It just proves they can sense something important is happening there. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound too strange when I say that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers. It's perfectly normal to notice when people are paying attention to your hard work, isn't it? I just want to do a good job for the community.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't feel like you had a special role or mission in life?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose so. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF30.9, F34.0, F60.0\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hello, Doctor. Thank you for seeing me, I really appreciate your time. I hope I'm not a bother to you today. \n\nWell, um... my family is actually the one who wanted me here. They're a little upset with me, I suppose. They say I’m here because I... well, I dipped into our savings a bit. It was just to buy some wood, and a bunch of lights, and some display stuff for the yard. I know it probably sounds irresponsible to them, but I was just trying to do something really nice. \n\nI kept pushing the work after dark, just trying to get it all set up, because... well, I felt like if I stopped, the whole house, and really the whole block, would just lose its chance. It felt like such an important thing to finish for everyone's sake. I was just trying to do something good for the neighborhood, you know? I hope you don't think badly of me for wanting to help out... does that make sense, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Did you feel like your yard display was going to be the best on the block or even in the whole town?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I really don't want to sound boastful, Doctor. I know nobody likes a show-off, and I promise I'm not trying to be one. But... yes, I suppose I did think that. \n\nI honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something really important. You know, something people would actually travel just to come see. I just... well, I feel like nobody else really understands light and pictures the exact way I do. I can just see how it all goes together so perfectly in my head, and I wanted to share that gift. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound too arrogant of me to say that out loud to you... is it okay to feel a little proud of a talent like that? I just wanted to do a good job.\n\nDoctor: Have you always been someone who comes up with big projects like this, or is this a relatively new thing for you?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose I've always liked to tinker a bit, you know? Just little things around the house. But... no, I wouldn't say I usually take on things quite this big. \n\nI mean, I'm 73, Doctor. I usually just like to sit on the porch and enjoy a quiet afternoon. I don't want you to think I'm always running around causing a fuss or spending money we don't have. I'm usually very careful, I promise. \n\nIt's just... lately, this idea just felt so right, and I had so much energy for it. I didn't even feel tired, even when I was working late. It just felt like a special occasion, I guess. I hope that's alright? I mean, it's not a bad thing to find a new passion at my age, is it?\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like your ideas or abilities are superior to others?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no. I really hope I haven't given you the impression that I'm some sort of... well, a know-it-all. I would hate for you to think that about me. \n\nI honestly don't think I'm better than anyone else. I mean, I didn't even finish high school, so I've always known I'm not the smartest fellow in the room. Usually, I'm just happy to take a back seat and listen to what other folks have to say. \n\nIt's really just... just this one time, with the yard and the lights, that I felt like I had a special touch for something. But normally? No, Doctor, I would never look down on my neighbors or think I'm superior to anybody. I've always tried to be a good, humble person. I hope you believe me when I say that?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any other changes in your mood or behavior over the past few months, aside from your enthusiasm for the yard project?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I suppose there have been a few little things, but nothing too terrible, I hope. I mean, mostly I've just been feeling... well, really wonderful, to be honest. Most days I wake up feeling so charged up, almost joyful, you know? Like I'm a young man again, ready to take on the world. It's been a lovely feeling, really. \n\nBut... oh, I hate to admit this, Doctor, because I really do try to be a patient man... I guess I have been a little short-tempered lately. Just... just when people don't understand what I'm trying to do. Like if my wife asks too many questions about the project, or those contractors I hired try to tell me something can't be done... I just get so hot, so fast. \n\nI've even yelled a few times, and I feel just awful about it afterwards. I know it's not polite to raise your voice, and I really don't want to be a difficult person to be around. Do you think it's normal to get a little frustrated when you have a clear vision and people keep getting in the way? I'm trying to be better about it, I really am.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you didn't feel wonderful or charged up?\n\nPatient: Well, um... no, not really. I mean, I've been feeling pretty good overall. I don't want you to think I'm sitting around moping or anything like that. I've always tried to keep a positive attitude, you know? \n\nI suppose... well, everyone has a quiet day now and then, right? Maybe a day where I just sit and rest a bit more, but nothing bad. I certainly haven't been feeling down or depressed, if that's what you mean. I'm usually very cheerful. I hope that's the right answer? I just wouldn't want you to worry that I'm not taking care of myself emotionally. I've been doing just fine in that department, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have a special role or mission in life that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I know people probably think I'm a bit mixed up in the head right now, and I really hope you don't think I'm crazy for saying this. I just... I want to be honest with you, because you've been so kind. \n\nI don't think I'm a prophet or anything grand like that, I promise. But... well, I am quite sure that our neighborhood was picked for something special. Like... like it's meant to be the site of my last big civic art piece. The yard, the lights, all of it. It's supposed to bring everyone together, I think. \n\nAnd I know it sounds silly, an old man talking like this, but... I see little signs that other people know it, too. Like when the delivery trucks slow down as they drive past, or when the neighbors peek over the fence. They don't say anything, but I can tell they understand something important is happening. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too strange? I just feel like I'm finally doing something that really matters, and I'd hate for you to think I'm just losing my marbles. Do you... do you think it's okay to feel that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have any special powers or abilities that set you apart from others?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... \"powers\" sounds a bit like a comic book, doesn't it? I wouldn't use a word like that, I promise. I don't want you to think I've lost touch with reality or believe I can fly or anything silly like that. \n\nIt's just... well, I hope this doesn't sound too boastful. I was always taught to be humble, and I really try not to brag, Doctor, I do. But... I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something really important. Something that folks will actually travel from all over just to come see. \n\nAnd it's mostly because... well, I've just realized that nobody understands light and pictures quite the way I do. It all just makes perfect sense to me right now, clear as a bell. I have this... this eye for it, I suppose, that sets me apart when it comes to this work. \n\nI hope it's alright to say that? I really don't want to come across as conceited to you, I'd hate for you to think poorly of me. I just feel so certain about what I can build. It's perfectly normal to be a little proud when you discover you have a special talent for something, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel like your yard project is going to be something that people will travel to see?\n\nPatient: Well, yes... I do still feel that way. I mean, I hope you don't think I'm being too big for my britches, saying it again. I know I shouldn't toot my own horn too much, and I really try to be modest, I do. \n\nBut... yes, I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something truly important. Something folks will want to come see from all over. It's just... well, like I mentioned before, I just feel like nobody else understands how to put the light and the pictures together quite the way I do right now. It's like a gift, I suppose. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm being arrogant? I'd hate for you to think I'm just an old man full of hot air. I just feel so sure about it, and I want to be honest with you. It's okay to have a little faith in your own abilities, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you think you're destined for greatness or have a special connection to something bigger than yourself?\n\nPatient: Oh, \"greatness\"... that's a very strong word, Doctor. I really hope I'm not giving you the impression that I think I'm some sort of famous celebrity or a king or anything. I'd be so embarrassed if you thought I was getting a big head. I was always taught that pride comes before a fall, you know?\n\nIt's really just like I was trying to explain a moment ago. I know I keep repeating myself, and I'm so sorry if I'm talking your ear off about it. But... I just feel tied to that Schiller Park house project. I don't think I'm destined to rule the world, but I do honestly believe I'm meant to turn that specific property into something truly important. \n\nNot because *I'm* great, but just because... well, like I was saying, the way I see the lights and the pictures in my mind right now, it's just so perfectly clear. Nobody else quite has that same vision or understands it the way I do right now. And I just want to put it together so people can come see it and feel it, too. \n\nDoes that make sense to you? I just want to do something beautiful for the community while I still can. I really hope you don't think I'm being too full of myself for feeling that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you still think about your yard project and its potential impact on the community every day?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, Doctor, I do think about it every day. I really hope you don't mind me talking about it so much. I know my family and some of the folks around here think I'm a little... mixed up, maybe. And I'd hate for you to think I'm just an old fool causing a fuss for no reason. I always try to be a good neighbor, I really do.\n\nBut... I'm just so sure that this neighborhood was picked for my last big civic art piece. I don't mean that in a boastful way, I promise. It's just... well, I can tell people know about it, too. Sometimes a delivery truck will slow down right as it goes past the house, or I'll catch a neighbor looking over the fence. It just proves they can sense something important is happening there. \n\nI hope it doesn't sound too strange when I say that? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right answers. It's perfectly normal to notice when people are paying attention to your hard work, isn't it? I just want to do a good job for the community.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you didn't feel like you had a special role or mission in life?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose so. I mean, I haven't always felt quite this... connected to things, if that makes sense. I hope you don't think I've been walking around my whole life thinking I was on some grand crusade. I've always just tried to be a regular, hardworking fellow, you know? Pay my bills, keep my nose clean. \n\nBefore I got so charged up with these remodeling plans for the house, I wasn't really... well, I wasn't seeing any special meaning in the delivery trucks slowing down or the neighbors looking over the fence. Those sorts of things... those signs, I guess you could call them... they really only started happening once I felt so driven to turn the Schiller Park house into my big civic project. \n\nIt was like a switch flipped, and suddenly it all just became so clear to me. But before that, no, I didn't feel like I had a special mission. I was just an ordinary old man. I hope it's okay that I feel this way now? I just want to make sure I'm not sounding too foolish to you. It's alright for a person to find their true calling a little later in life, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you still believe you have a unique understanding of light and pictures that sets you apart from others?\n\nPatient: Well... yes, I suppose I do still believe that. I really hope that doesn't make me sound arrogant or... or boastful. I know I don't have a fancy education or anything, and I'd hate for you to think I'm putting on airs. \n\nIt's just... I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something truly important. A place people will actually want to come and see. And the reason I feel so sure about it is... well, it's because nobody else understands the light and the pictures the way I do right now. It's all just so vivid in my mind, and I know exactly how to put it together. \n\nI just want to share it with everyone. That's not a bad thing to want, is it? I hope you understand I'm just trying to do something nice with this... this understanding I have. You don't think I'm getting too big for my britches, do you, Doctor?\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you felt sad or not like your usual self?\n\nPatient: Oh... no, Doctor, I haven't been sad at all lately. I hope you don't mind me saying, but I've actually been feeling quite... well, quite good, really. Very positive. \n\nI always try my best to keep a smile on my face and stay focused on the work I need to do. I wouldn't want to be a burden to my family or to you by moping around or feeling sorry for myself, you know? So, no, no days like that at all. I promise I'm doing just fine in that department. \n\nIt's... it's a good thing to feel so upbeat, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing as well as you'd hope.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days recently where you didn't feel wonderful or charged up, Doctor?\n\nPatient: Well... no, Doctor, I really haven't. I can't think of a single day lately where I didn't feel just... well, full of pep and ready to go. Every morning I wake up feeling wonderful, just completely charged up to get to work on the house. \n\nI haven't had any slow or sluggish days at all. I hope it's alright that I've been running on all cylinders like this? I know a lot of fellows my age just sit around in a recliner and let the days pass them by, but I'm trying my absolute best to stay active and make the most of this energy. \n\nThat's a good thing, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm doing exactly what I ought to be doing to stay healthy, and I'd hate for you to think I'm overdoing it. I'm just trying to be a good patient and keep a positive attitude for you.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having any trouble sleeping at night with all this energy and these ideas?\n\nPatient: Oh... no, Doctor, no trouble at all. I know how important a good night's rest is, so I always make sure to go to bed and get my sleep. I wouldn't want you to worry that I'm running myself ragged or... or being irresponsible with my health, you know? \n\nI just close my eyes and rest, and when I wake up, I feel just wonderful and ready for the day. I hope that's exactly what you want to hear? I'm really trying my best to take good care of myself and be a good patient for you. I'm doing the right thing by getting my rest, aren't I?\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, Doctor... I don't want to sound boastful, you know? I was always taught it's not polite to brag. But... well, yes, sir. I really do. 

I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something really important. The kind of place people will actually come out just to see. And it's because... well, it's just that nobody understands light and pictures the way I do. I just have an eye for how it all fits together perfectly. 

I hope it's alright for me to say that out loud to you? I'm not trying to sound full of myself or anything, I promise. I just want to be completely honest with you. It's a good thing to have a special vision for a project at my age, isn't it? I'm just trying to make something beautiful.

Well, I... I know it probably sounds a bit silly for a man my age to talk like this. I don't want you to think I'm getting a big head or losing touch with reality, Doctor.

I just wouldn't want you to worry that I'm not taking care of myself emotionally. I've been doing just fine in that department, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have a special role or mission in life that others don't understand?\n\nPatient: Well, I... I know people probably think I'm a bit mixed up in the head right now, and I really hope you don't think I'm crazy for saying this. I just... I want to be honest with you, because you've been so kind. \n\nI don't think I'm a prophet or anything grand like that, I promise. But... well, I am quite sure that our neighborhood was picked for something special. Like... like it's meant to be the site of my last big civic art piece. The yard, the lights, all of it. It's supposed to bring everyone together, I think. \n\nAnd I know it sounds silly, an old man talking like this, but... I see little signs that other people know it, too. Like when the delivery trucks slow down as they drive past, or when the neighbors peek over the fence. They don't say anything, but I can tell they understand something important is happening. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound too strange? I just feel like I'm finally doing something that really matters, and I'd hate for you to think I'm just losing my marbles. Do you... do you think it's okay to feel that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you believe you have any special powers or abilities that set you apart from others?\n\nPatient: Oh, my... \"powers\" sounds a bit like a comic book, doesn't it? I wouldn't use a word like that, I promise. I don't want you to think I've lost touch with reality or believe I can fly or anything silly like that. \n\nIt's just... well, I hope this doesn't sound too boastful. I was always taught to be humble, and I really try not to brag, Doctor, I do. But... I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something really important. Something that folks will actually travel from all over just to come see. \n\nAnd it's mostly because... well, I've just realized that nobody understands light and pictures quite the way I do. It all just makes perfect sense to me right now, clear as a bell. I have this... this eye for it, I suppose, that sets me apart when it comes to this work. \n\nI hope it's alright to say that? I really don't want to come across as conceited to you, I'd hate for you to think poorly of me. I just feel so certain about what I can build. It's perfectly normal to be a little proud when you discover you have a special talent for something, isn't it?\n\nDoctor: Do you still feel like your yard project is going to be something that people will travel to see?\n\nPatient: Well, yes... I do still feel that way. I mean, I hope you don't think I'm being too big for my britches, saying it again. I know I shouldn't toot my own horn too much, and I really try to be modest, I do. \n\nBut... yes, I honestly believe I can turn that old Schiller Park house into something truly important. Something folks will want to come see from all over. I just want to do something beautiful for the community while I still can. I really hope you don't think I'm being too full of myself for feeling that way?\n\nDoctor: Do you still think about your yard project and its potential impact on the community every day?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, Doctor, I do think about it every day. I've always just tried to be a regular, hardworking fellow, you know? Well, um... for the most part, yes, sir. I'd say most days I wake up feeling just... well, almost joyful, really. Like I've got all this energy and I'm a young man again. It's a wonderful feeling to have a purpose, you know?
